  `docs/research/literature/ai/annotations/kowalski2023competition/kowalski2023competition.md`
  for fixed seed, repeated-game, mirrored-seat, reproducibility,
  resource-budget, and CCG competition-harness claims within that
  annotation's limits.
  Future specs may cite
  `docs/research/literature/ai/annotations/xi2023mastering/xi2023mastering.md`
  for long-term OSFP/self-play, deck/context-conditioned policy,
  legal-action masking, historical-opponent self-play, and
  resource-budget evaluation claims within that annotation's limits.
- The completed annotations support a hidden-info-safe ISMCTS /
  ensemble-determinization search baseline as a candidate, not an
  immediate implementation order. They do not justify hidden-
  information cheating, neural self-play, AlphaZero-style transfer, or
  Nash/equilibrium claims.
- The shared `docs/research/bibliography/references.bib` contains the
  aligned citekeys `cowling2012ismcts`, `cowling2012ensemble`,
  `long2010understanding`, `zinkevich2007regret`, and
  `lanctot2009monte`, `huang2022invalidmasking`,
  `timbers2022approxexploitability`, `herbrich2006trueskill`,
  `glickman1995glicko`,
  `dockhorn2019introducinghearthstoneaicompetition`,
  `kowalski2023competition`, and `xi2023mastering`, but bibliography
  entries are not a substitute for annotation.
- The two manuscript folders are placeholders only. The Gwent AI paper
  and theory paper should not make strong claims until annotations and
  benchmark/search results support them.
- Source PDFs are local-only by design. Future contributors who
  pull the repo will not have the same local PDFs unless they
  retrieve them themselves; this is intentional licensing safety,
  not a defect.

## Active Risks (cEp13 implemented status)

- cEp13 is a product presentation asset/CSS phase only. Row overlays are derived from
  public Weather-zone cards and do not compute or alter scoring, legal moves,
  command execution, prompts, round resolution, or AI.
- The runtime weather overlay assets are now user-converted SVGs at `0.8`
  opacity. The older cEp13 PNG strips remain in the repository as provenance /
  fallback assets unless a later cleanup explicitly removes them.
- Multiple weather effects can stack on the same row. Skellige Storm is rendered
  as its own distinct effect and later in the stack, but no special blended
  combination art exists yet.
- The browser smoke covers playing one weather card and observing affected row
  overlays. It does not exhaustively click through every weather source in one
  run.

## Active Risks (cEp12 implemented status)

- cEp12 is a product presentation phase only. Drag/drop dispatches exact move
  ids already present on cEp11 selected-card target buttons; it does not compute
  legality from geometry.
- Card-flight remains UI-only. Animation cleanup timers and animation-end
  handlers remove presentation layers only; no timer advances rules, AI,
  prompts, round resolution, or game end.
- Hidden-info safety remains a hard boundary: AI hand/deck identities are not
  draggable, previewed, or animated from hidden origins.
- Touch dragging is intentionally deferred. Touch users keep the existing tap
  and click controls.
- Browser smoke directly covers row and selected-weather drag/drop, invalid
  drop no-op, right-click no-preview, card-flight, and hidden-info assertions.
  Unit view-model coverage locks row/card/horn/weather drag move ids and
  fallback/no-target exclusion. Full Decoy bounce choreography, card-instance
  browser paths, and row-horn browser paths remain follow-ups.
- cEp12 preserves the cEp11/cEp11.1 browser smokes, including weather
  targeting, match-end ledger/rematch, and no nested interactive board rows.

## Active Risks (cEp11.1 implemented status)

- cEp11.1 is test/docs-only. It intentionally did not change engine rules,
  legal moves, scoring, prompts, AI policy, Redux adapter behavior, catalog
  data, production UI behavior, product layout/styling, or route defaults.
- The match-end smoke is a pass-only product tripwire with a hard four-round
  limit. It proves the ledger branch, real history/standing fields, Escape
  persistence, and rematch flow, but it is not a broad strategic full-match
  automation suite.
- The match-end setup/change-deck branch is still not covered by a dedicated
  browser click-through. cEp11.1 covered rematch as the clean route branch and
  documents the omission in the phase report.
- Both cEp11.1 smokes keep the existing hidden-info posture: visible text must
  not expose raw runtime instance IDs, raw `sourceId` labels, or hidden AI
  hand/deck identities.

## Active Risks (cEp11 status)

- cEp11 is a product presentation phase only. `buildSelectedCardTargetViewModel` consumes the selected human hand card, the legal `PlayCardMove[]` for that selection, and a public visible-card lookup. It never computes legality, never invents targets, and never dispatches commands.
- The board row container is now `<div data-testid="authentic-board-row">`; post-review cEp13 removes the old visible row-level `play here` button and moves the legal row target/drop metadata onto the row itself through `data-row-target="true"` and `data-drop-move-id`. Board-card targets remain `<button data-testid="authentic-board-card-target">` for `card_instance` moves like Decoy, but the old visible `choose` badge is removed. The DOM-level invariant that board rows do not become `<button>` containers is covered by the Playwright Chromium smoke (`authentic match avoids nested interactive elements on board rows (cEp11)`).
- Selecting Commander's Horn now marks an icon-only reserved horn slot per legal own row inside the row's card strip (`authentic-board-row-horn-target`); the slot is a `<div>` carrying the exact legal `data-drop-move-id`, not a visible target button or label, and the placed-card frame is 8px wider/taller than the card. Score-card leader state labels are plain inline text rather than bordered tags. Selecting a weather card highlights the Weather panel itself as `authentic-weather-target` with `data-drop-move-id`, without a nested `play weather` button. Global/no-target plays remain in the right-rail as compact `authentic-target-action` buttons, which is the only place those cards can be dispatched. `groupTargetActions` from the diagnostic engine shell helper is no longer consumed by the authentic match UI but remains exported for the diagnostic engine shell.
- Target labels never include raw `seat_a:...` / `seat_b:...` instance IDs or hidden opponent source IDs. `cardLabel` falls back to a public `card` literal when a card target is missing from the visible-card lookup, so opponents' hidden-info safety holds even when the visible-card lookup is incomplete (which today never happens, because the lookup is built from public board / hand / weather / discard cards only). The unit tests assert this on every user-facing label field.
- The dedicated weather-target browser smoke is now covered by cEp11.1 using a browser-local fixture deck and bounded seed search. cEp11 itself remains the product implementation phase for the spatial target surface.

## Active Risks (cEp10 status)

- cEp10 is a product presentation phase only. `buildMatchLedgerViewModel` and `buildResolveRoundActionViewModel` consume `RoundResult[]`, the engine `winner`, public seat gem counts, the match phase, and the legal-move flags exposed through the engine selectors. Neither helper computes scoring, gem loss, winner, or next starter; all of those fields are read directly off engine state.
- The dismissed-overlay key embeds `activeMatchKey` (which itself embeds `matchPresentationRun`), so rematch and setup re-entries cannot leave a stale dismissal cache that hides a fresh round overlay. `setDismissedRoundOverlayKey(null)` is also called from `startNewGame`, `returnToPreGame`, and the active-setup-key effect as belt-and-suspenders.
- Match-end `Escape` dismissal is intentionally not wired. Match-end navigation (`rematch` / `change deck` / `close`) is only reachable through the explicit ledger buttons, which keeps a final-state dismissal from leaving the user at a frozen board with no obvious next action. Non-game-end `Escape` dismisses the round ledger as expected.
- Click-outside is intentionally inert across all match modals (`LookThreeCardsRevealDialog`, `DiscardBrowser`, inspect modals, round/match ledger). Adding it asymmetrically to one ledger would surprise users and could silently dismiss the match-end summary.
- Browser smoke continues to cover the cEp9 leader/prompt surfaces. Driving a full Bo3 to game end remains a planned follow-up so the match-end ledger fields and navigation get Playwright coverage on top of the new unit tests.

## Active Risks (cEp9 status)

- cEp9 is a product presentation phase only. `buildLeaderStatusViewModel` uses public leader state, `cancelledThisRound`, phase/turn/pass/prompt flags, and the legal leader move count; it intentionally does not duplicate target-availability rules from the engine.
- Suppression presentation depends on the selector-provided `leader.cancelledThisRound` flag, which is derived from `seat.leaderCancelledRound === state.round` in engine state. The UI does not infer suppression from the event log.
- Prompt option labels are built from legal `choose_prompt_option` moves only. For non-owner prompts, the prompt presentation view model returns no options, so AI-owned deck/hand prompt option details stay hidden.
- The cEp9 prompt copy covers the implemented prompt families listed in the spec. Unknown prompt kinds still fall back to generic prompt text so future abilities can render safely before a dedicated product-copy pass.
- Browser coverage was added to existing weather-choice and look-three-card smokes. Direct hidden-info assertions for non-owner deck prompt options are unit-level; a broader browser seed suite for every prompt family remains a possible Cluster E regression follow-up.

## Active Risks (cCp29 implemented status)

- **The official leader matrix is complete.** Active executable leader count is now **14**, passive count remains **7**, setup-time count remains **1**, combined implemented union is **22**, and placeholder count is **0**. `OfficialLeaderPromotionManifest.placeholderLeaderAbilityIds` is the empty list. Tranche 4 closes with cCp29.
- cCp29 is the first explicit cross-cutting suppression phase. The new `SeatState.leaderCancelledRound: number | null` field threads through `getLeaderMove` (active leader gate), `getWeatherPolicyBySeat` / `getRowHornPolicyBySeat` / `getDoubleSpiesPolicyBySeat` / `hasRandomMedicPolicyForSeat` (passive scoring/ability gates). Suppression is current-round only and clears at round transition (`resolveRoundEnd` resets both seats). The helper `isLeaderSuppressedThisRound({ state, seatId })` is the single source of truth and ignores stale prior-round values defensively.
- The reaction-prompt gate inside `executeLeader` runs **before** the per-leader branches dispatch, so any non-`cancel_leader` active leader resolved while the opposing seat owns an unused, unsuppressed `cancel_leader` opens a `choose_option` reaction prompt instead of resolving its effect. The decline path replays the attempted leader through `executeLeader(input, { bypassCancelReaction: true, skipLegalCheck: true })` so the same per-leader branch fires exactly once without re-opening the prompt. The reaction-cancel path emits both `leader_used` events (White Flame and the cancelled target) and hands off the turn from the attempted leader seat.
- Hidden-info contract is preserved: leader identity, leader-used status, and current-round suppression are all public. AI seat observation and safe simulation export expose `cancelledThisRound: boolean` flags for both seats; the reaction prompt's captured original target lives only in `pendingPrompt.context.leaderCancel.target` (typed `unknown`) and is never serialized or replayed outside the engine. Recent activity / event summaries render `leader_cancelled` as a count-only public label.
- The new `PendingPromptStage` value `"leader_cancel_reaction"`, `PendingPromptContext.leaderCancel`, `LeaderCancelReactionContext`, and `GameEvent.leader_cancelled` shape are additive on existing unions; existing consumers ignore them. The `executeLeader` `ExecuteLeaderOptions` (`bypassCancelReaction`, `skipLegalCheck`) is private to `commands.ts` and only used by the decline-replay path.
- AI heuristic is bounded: `legal-heuristic-v0` deterministically picks `cancel-leader:cancel` when it owns a reaction prompt; the proactive AI heuristic (`isUsefulLeaderMove`) does not gate `cancel_leader` proactively, so the AI does not call White Flame proactively in practice. Future heuristic upgrades can value the proactive use against current-round opponent passives without breaking the reaction-prompt path.
- Tests added: `tests/game/coreCancelLeader.test.ts` (66 focused tests covering metadata / manifest / helpers / proactive legal moves and command / round transition / passive suppression / legal-move guard for every implemented active leader / reaction prompt open / cancel option / decline option / AI / observation / export / UI hooks). Existing tests updated to drop placeholder-leader fixtures and reflect the cCp29 totals.

## Active Risks (cCp28 implemented status)

- cCp28 is the first explicit hidden-info disclosure phase. The hidden-info contract is preserved: the acting seat sees the revealed opponent hand cards through `pendingPrompt.context.revealedCardIds` only while the acknowledgement prompt is open. The non-acting seat keeps `pendingPrompt: null` (covered by `getPromptMoves`, `seatObservation.buildSeatObservation`, and `buildSafeSimulationObservation`); revealed names, source IDs, and raw instance IDs do not leak through UI, AI observation, recent activity, or simulation export. Recent activity / event summaries render count-only labels ("Human looked at 3 opponent hand cards") even though the underlying `opponent_hand_revealed` event payload carries `cardIds` and `sourceIds` for the internal log.
- The reveal lives only inside the pending acknowledgement prompt. After acknowledgement, `state.pendingPrompt` is cleared and no `MatchState` field retains the snapshot; the engine cannot be queried to re-derive the revealed identities and the UI cannot reopen the modal. This follows Settled Product Decision §6.
- The leader is consumed at `UseLeader` (`seat.leaderUsed = true` is set there, not at acknowledgement). The acknowledgement prompt blocks the turn (no `handoffTurn` while `state.pendingPrompt` is non-null) and does **not** re-consume the leader. A repeated `UseLeader` while the acknowledgement is pending rejects through the standard `prompt_pending` rule guard; a second `UseLeader` after acknowledgement is illegal because `seat.leaderUsed === true`.
- RNG policy is explicit and tested: hand length 1, 2, or 3 reveals every card in opponent hand order **without invoking RNG** (state.rng.state is preserved); hand length > 3 selects 3 cards uniformly without replacement using `createSeededRngFromState` + `shuffleWithRng` and advances `state.rng.state` to the post-shuffle RNG state. The reveal display order is the intersection of opponent hand order with the chosen subset, so the visible reveal order is deterministic and matches opponent hand order.
- Revealed cards are not moved out of opponent hand and do not trigger their abilities. No `card_moved` event is emitted for revealed cards. No on-play effects (Scorch, weather, Medic, Spy, Avenger, Summon, Muster, Mardroeme, Berserker) fire on reveal. Off-owner cards in opponent hand are eligible and preserve owner / controller / zone.
- The new `PendingPromptTarget` variant `{ kind: "none" }`, `PendingPromptStage` value `"opponent_hand_reveal"`, `PendingPromptContext.revealedCardIds`, and `GameEvent.opponent_hand_revealed` shape are additive on existing unions. UI / inspector / view-model code that previously narrowed `PendingPromptTarget["kind"]` was extended for exhaustiveness in `seatObservation.buildSeatObservation`, `exportObservation.buildSafeSimulationObservation`, and `legalMoves.getPromptMoves`. The new `UseLeaderMove.metadata.opponentHandCount` and `revealCount` diagnostic fields are optional; existing consumers ignore them.

## Active Risks (cCp27 implemented status)

- Tranche 2 was **complete after cCp25**, Tranche 3 was opened by
  cCp26's `draw_extra_card` setup-time leader, and Tranche 3 is now
  **complete after cCp27** with the engine's first true multi-stage
  prompt leader (`discard_two_draw_one_from_deck` on Eredin: Destroyer
  of Worlds). Tranche 4 (`look_three_cards`, `cancel_leader`) is the
  only remaining tranche.
- The cCp27 implementation introduced two new `LegalMoveTarget` kinds
  (`card_instance_set`, `deck_card_instance`) and matching
  `PendingPromptTarget` shapes plus `PendingPromptStage` and optional
  `stage` / `context` fields on `PendingPrompt`. UI / inspector / view-
  model code that previously narrowed `LegalMoveTarget["kind"]` was
  updated for exhaustiveness in `playMoveHelpers.describePlayTarget`
  and `engineShellViewModels.TARGET_GROUP_LABELS`. No production
  consumer hard-codes the union beyond these helpers; future leaders
  that introduce more target kinds should update these sites.
- Hidden-info contract is preserved: `getPromptMoves` returns `[]` for
  the non-acting seat at both stages; `buildSeatObservation` returns
  `pendingPrompt: null` to the wrong seat; simulation export
  observation surfaces the acting seat's own deck card via a
  prompt-local safe ref `own_deck_option_<index>`. Action encoding
  falls back to `none` for both new target kinds — the prompt option's
  safe ref lives on the observation side, not the action side.
- The leader is consumed only after stage 2 resolves successfully.
  Between stage 1 and stage 2, the engine state is partially resolved:
  selected hand cards are already in the acting discard,
  `state.pendingPrompt` points at the stage 2 deck-draw prompt, and
  the acting seat still owns the turn with `seat.leaderUsed === false`.
  Tests cover this state explicitly. Any non-prompt command attempted
  during the pending stage 2 prompt rejects through the existing
  `prompt_pending` rule guard.
- Discarded hand cards and drawn deck cards do **not** trigger card
  abilities. Stage 1 keeps the discard transaction strictly
  hand→discard with `card_moved.reason === "leader_discard_for_draw"`
  and never invokes `resolveCardAbilities` or any battlefield
  discard-trigger helper (Summon, Avenger, Medic, Spy, Scorch,
  weather, Muster, Berserker, Mardroeme, Skellige round-three queue).
  Stage 2 keeps the draw transaction strictly deck→hand with
  `card_moved.reason === "leader_draw_from_deck"` and never invokes
  `resolveCardAbilities` on the drawn card.
- Deterministic shuffle: stage 2's remaining-deck shuffle uses
  `createSeededRngFromState(state.rng.seed, state.rng.state)` plus
  `shuffleWithRng` on the post-draw deck. RNG advances only when the
  remaining deck has ≥ 2 cards; deck length 0 or 1 is a deterministic
  no-op and `state.rng.state` is preserved. The engine emits
  `deck_shuffled` with `reason === "leader_discard_draw"` regardless,
  so the event log records that the leader's shuffle step completed.
- Active executable leader count is now **12**, passive count remains
  **7**, setup-time count remains **1**, combined implemented union is
  **20**, and placeholder count is **2** (`cancel_leader`,
  `look_three_cards`).

## Active Risks (cCp26 implemented status)

- Tranche 2 is **complete**, and Tranche 3 is **in progress** after cCp26's `draw_extra_card` landing.
- `draw_extra_card` is **setup-time**, not passive and not active. The new manifest bucket `implementedSetupLeaderSourceIds` records this distinctly so future setup-time leaders (none currently planned) can join without polluting passive scoring or active executable sets.
- The new helper module `src/game/core/leaderSetup.ts` is the single source of truth for setup-time draw-count adjustments. Future setup-time leaders (or any phase that adjusts hand size at `startMatch`) should extend that module rather than scattering string checks through `setup.ts`. The helper is pure, testable, and faction/name-agnostic — it switches purely on the catalog ability ID.
- The 11-card opening hand creates more legal one-card mulligan options on the first mulligan tick (12 total: 1 keep + 11 single-card mulligans), but the cap of two total redraws per seat is unchanged. After two redraws, the seat's `mulliganComplete` flips to true and emits no further legal mulligan moves, identical to the existing 10-card flow.
- No `Math.random` was introduced. The 11th card is the next deterministic card off the existing seeded-RNG-shuffled deck top. The setup determinism contract is preserved: same seed and deck list yields byte-for-byte identical setup state and events; when only the leader differs, Daisy's first 10 hand cards equal the equivalent non-Daisy seat's first 10 cards exactly, and Daisy's 11th hand card is the next card that would otherwise have remained on top of the non-Daisy seat's deck.
- Tiny test decks shorter than 11 cards do not throw under Daisy; the existing `slice` behavior draws available cards. Production decks always have enough cards for an 11-card initial draw.
- AI heuristic policy is unchanged. `legal-heuristic-v0` consumes the 11-card hand through the existing legal-move generator without code change. Future AI tuning could weight Daisy's extra-card advantage explicitly, but no current heuristic surface depends on hand size.
- Hidden-info contract is preserved: hand-size disclosure is already part of the public observation surface (every seat's hand count is visible to the opponent), so the extra card does not create a new disclosure. Raw engine setup transactions still include internal initial-hand card IDs for each seat, but cCp26 adds no new public observation, UI label, AI observation, recent-activity summary, or simulation export field.
- The setup helper's lookup is faction-agnostic — it switches purely on `leader.ability === "draw_extra_card"`. A defensive synthetic-catalog test confirms the helper checks the catalog's `ability` field rather than just trusting the source ID.
- Active executable leader count remains **11**, passive count remains **7**, and the new setup-time count is **1**. Combined implemented union is **19**; placeholder count drops to **3**.

## Active Risks (cCp25 implemented status)

- Tranche 2 is **complete** as of cCp25.
- The shared `placeMedicRevival` helper inside `src/game/core/abilities.ts` is the single source of truth for Medic placement (Spy redirection, controller reset, `card_played`, child ability resolution, Mardroeme settlement). Future Medic refactors should keep adding behavior to that helper rather than splitting it.
- Hidden-info contract is preserved: own discard is already public, and the engine event log still only reveals the actually revived card (no leak of unchosen candidate IDs through `summarizeEvents` or simulation export).
- Hero immunity (§17.1) is the only invariant that pivots between random and prompt-based Medic. A hypothetical future hero source with the `medic` ability must keep the prompt path; this is enforced by a test-local hero Medic fixture in `tests/game/coreRandomMedicLeader.test.ts`.

## Active Risks (cCp23 status)

- cCp23 introduced the engine's first runtime discard-to-deck recycle and runtime deck shuffle. Implementation reuses `createSeededRngFromState` + `shuffleWithRng` and does not hand-roll shuffle logic; one `SeededRng` instance is shared across the whole command, with `state.rng.state` advanced from `rng.getState()` at the end.
- The rule shuffles only affected seats whose discard piles contributed cards. Empty-discard seats' hidden deck order is preserved (no hidden-order mutation for a no-op) and emit no `deck_shuffled` event.
- Cards recycle into the deck for the discard pile seat, not necessarily their immutable owner. Off-owner cards (Spy-like cards in the opposite discard pile) preserve `owner` while resetting `controller` and `zone.seat` to the destination deck seat. The cCp23 test suite covers the off-owner case explicitly.
- The new event reasons `card_moved.reason === "leader_shuffle_into_deck"` and `deck_shuffled.reason === "leader_shuffle_into_deck"` do not expose post-shuffle deck order. UI, AI observation, and simulation/export summaries continue to treat deck order as hidden information.
- Baseline `legal-heuristic-v0` does not strategically use Crach in cCp23. The rule is legal and executable, but AI timing/tuning is deferred. Future AI tranches could weight Crach by acting-seat discard composition and remaining deck size; the cCp23 implementation does not change this surface.
- Skellige round-three return §17.18 still operates on whatever is in discard at future round-end; Crach does not create a permanent return pool.

## Active Risks (cCp22 status)

- cCp22 closes the `restore_discard_to_hand` leader rule as an active one-shot leader resolved through a single-step `choose_card` prompt over the acting seat's own discard pile. **`PendingPromptOption.target.row` is now optional** — Medic prompts continue to set `row`, the new restore prompt omits it, and `getPromptMoves` builds `card_instance` legal-move targets with or without `row` accordingly. UI consumers that destructure `target.row` should treat it as optional. The existing `playMoveHelpers.describePlayTarget` already handled the optional case.
- The cCp22 transaction keeps `currentTurn` on the acting seat while the restore prompt is pending. `seat.leaderUsed` flips to `true` and `leader_used` fires only after a legal `ChoosePromptOption` resolves; rejection paths (non-`none` `UseLeader` target, empty discard, wrong seat on prompt resolve, invalid option ID, stale target no longer in own discard, missing prompt) raise `EngineRuleError` and never consume the leader.
- The new `card_moved.reason === "leader_restore_discard_to_hand"` joins the engine event union. JSONL export and replay infrastructure inherits the new reason automatically through the typed union; the `motionByCardId` UI mapping in `AuthenticMatchScreen.tsx` falls through to its `to.kind === "discard"` and `to.kind === "hand"` defaults — restored cards land in hand, so no special motion class is registered (acceptable until a future Cluster E pass adds a "restored" motion).
- The generic `PromptPanel` renders the `choose_card` options as plain labelled buttons (`"Restore <card name> to hand"`); no new modal or card-tile UI was needed. Richer discard-card prompt tiles are intentionally deferred to a later Cluster E polish phase.
- AI-owned restore prompts remain hidden from the human UI: `getPromptMoves` returns `[]` for any seat that does not own the prompt, so the human's `promptMoves` array is empty for AI prompts; `summarizeEvents` only exposes `prompt.seatId` and `prompt.abilityId` for `prompt_opened` events, never option labels or card identities. Human-owned options are public because they come from the human player's own discard browser surface (already public per §13 / §17.5).
- The `legal-heuristic-v0` AI prompt policy ranks `choose_prompt_option` moves by `option.targetStrength ?? 0` with a deterministic moveId fallback. `seatObservation` derives `targetStrength` from the catalog source (`cardsBySourceId.get(option.target.sourceId)?.strength`), which is defined for every card kind (specials/weather have strength 0 by catalog convention). Restored special / weather options therefore rank below restored unit / hero options, but the `?? 0` fallback prevents crashes if the strength is undefined.
- All card kinds in own discard are eligible — units, heroes, specials, weather, and side-deck-only / generated cards. **Heroes are eligible** because the leader text says "card" without Medic's non-hero unit restriction. Restored cards return to hand and do **not** trigger their abilities; a restored Medic does not chain another revival, a restored Scorch does not fire on restore, a restored weather card does not enter the weather zone. They behave normally only if played later.

## Active Risks (cCp21 status)

- cCp21 closes the `optimize_agile_rows` leader rule as an active one-shot leader with auto-place + tied-row choice. The settled product decision §3 was refined from pure auto-place to "auto-place when uniquely best; expose tied best rows as legal player choices." The cEp8 leader-choice menu renders the choice through the existing `metadata.targetLabel` fallback chain; no new UI layout is needed.
- The cCp21 planner scores all candidate rows before filtering for executable movement. Pure no-op rows are not executable, and worse movable rows are not offered when the current no-op row is uniquely best, so the leader cannot be consumed to reduce the acting seat's current score.
- The new `metadata.targetRequirement === "agile_row_choice"` value is added to the `UseLeaderMove` metadata union. UI/inspector code that hard-codes the previous union variants (`"none" | "future_prompt" | "deck_weather_source"`) should be reviewed; the existing cEp8 menu only reads `targetLabel` / `targetCardName` / `sourceId` / `move.label`, so it is unaffected. No production UI consumer hard-codes the union today.
- The new `card_moved.reason === "leader_optimize_agile"` joins the engine event union. JSONL export and replay infrastructure inherits the new reason automatically through the typed union.
- The planning helper iterates a small candidate set (≤3 rows × cloned state) per call. For deck builds with many Agile units, the cost is bounded by the catalog row count and the eligible-card count; no state mutation occurs.
- Hero/Spy boundary behavior is exercised through both the production catalog (Kayran hero+agile, no production agile spy) and a test-local agile-Spy fixture (`test.cCp21.agile-spy`). If a future production Spy + Agile card is added, the helper already excludes it from eligibility because it sits on the opponent board side.
- cCp29 now implements `cancel_leader` as current-round suppression through `seat.leaderCancelledRound`. The `optimize_agile_rows` execution path remains free of passive policy lookups; suppression short-circuits the move at `getLeaderMove` before the helper runs.

## Active Risks (cCp20 status)

- cCp20 closes the `double_spies` leader rule as a whole-match passive, not a one-shot snapshot. The earlier "snapshot at fire time" framing from pre-cCp19 prose is now corrected in `docs/leader-ability-matrix.md` Pattern 2 and `docs/gwent-rules.md` §17.12e; historical reports under `audit/reports/` were not edited per the spec.
- cCp29 now implements `cancel_leader` as current-round suppression through `seat.leaderCancelledRound`. `getDoubleSpiesPolicyBySeat` now short-circuits suppressed seats before emitting the double-spies policy, mirroring the cCp19 row-horn and cCp15 weather-policy helpers.
- UI/inspector code should not hard-code a single horn-style modifier name; the score breakdown's `modifiers` list now carries `"commanders_horn"` (physical horn), `"leader_horn"` (cCp19 row horn), and `"leader_double_spies"` (cCp20). The existing cEp5.3 inspector renders the modifier list verbatim, so it is already compatible.
- The new `CardScoreEntry.spyMultiplier` and `CardScoreEntry.afterSpyMultiplier` fields are part of `MatchScoreBreakdown`. Existing consumers used `toMatchObject` or read named fields, so the field addition is compatible. Future consumers should be aware of the field for transparency UI.
- Catalog promotion is still untouched. Cow / Kambi remain `avenger`, Bovine Defense Force remains ability-less, and the combined Cow/Bovine staging candidate stays blocked.
- `getDoubleSpiesPolicyBySeat` selects by leader source identity, not faction. Faction does not gate the policy: a Monsters seat with any non-Treacherous Monsters leader does not enable double-spies, and a non-Monsters seat with the Treacherous leader (an unusual fixture) would enable it.

## Active Risks (cEp8 status)

- cEp8 closes the authentic match UI gap for multi-move leaders. The product flow now classifies `UseLeaderMove[]` through `buildLeaderActionViewModel` and surfaces a compact menu when more than one legal `use_leader` move is available; selecting an option dispatches the exact engine `UseLeader` command for that move's `target` and never reconstructs `deck_card_source` shapes from labels or catalog state.
- The choice UI is intentionally generic. Currently only `play_any_weather` (Eredin) emits multiple legal leader moves. When future leader rules (e.g. Eredin tutor, Emhyr discard/restore) emit multi-target legal moves with their own target shapes, the helper's label fallback (`targetCardName → targetLabel → sourceId → move.label`) keeps working without UI churn, but distinct target families (e.g. hand-source / discard-source) may want their own labelling refinements in later phases.
- The browser smoke uses a small fixed seed list (`cep8-eredin-1`..`cep8-eredin-5`) and a seeded localStorage Monsters/Eredin deck to find a deterministic state with at least two eligible weather sources still in deck after mulligan. The first listed seed currently passes; if engine deck-shuffle behavior changes or the local-deck schema migrates, the seed list may need to be revisited.

## Active Risks (cCp17 status)

- cCp17 closes the linked-side-deck discard-trigger work for the pure engine. Generic `summon` is now `implemented`; the Avenger ↔ Summon distinction is locked into both rules (`docs/gwent-rules.md` §17.10/§17.10b) and tests (`tests/game/coreSummonDiscardTrigger.test.ts` + the new metadata-status invariant in `linkedAbilityInvariants`).
- No new live `summon` source ships in the permanent catalog. Cow / Kambi remain `avenger`. Bovine Defense Force remains ability-less. The combined Cow/Bovine staging candidate stays blocked by `catalog_split_required:avenger` + `rule_gap:avenger`. The cCp17 focused test file uses small file-local catalog fixtures (`test.cCp17.summon-base` / `test.cCp17.summon-replacement`) so the resolver can be exercised end-to-end without polluting the production catalog.
- Replacements are summoned, not "played" — `resolveCardAbilities` is not invoked on the replacement. If a future Summon source's replacement ever needs an on-summon ability (similar to the Hemdall caveat for Avenger), the helper will need to opt into `resolveCardAbilities`. The current side-deck-only replacements (Bovine, Hemdall, Vildkaarl, Young Vildkaarl) all carry `abilities: ["none"]` or `morale_boost`/`tight_bond` board-state-only abilities, so this is not a present blocker.
- One `it.todo` remains in `tests/catalog/linkedAbilityInvariants.test.ts`, scoped to placeholder leader tutor/restore effects (Eredin, Emhyr, etc.). Promote it when the leader resolvers land.

## Active Risks (cCp11 status)

- cCp10 F-1, F-2, F-3 are resolved by cCp11.
- Avenger replacements pulled from the side deck during round cleanup persist into the next round; they are not eligible for Monsters keep (kept cards bypass the avenger trigger entirely).
- After cCp22, 7 of 22 leader records still report placeholder ability metadata. Fourteen leader ability records (clear_weather + four weather-pulling + weather_half_penalty + scorch_range + scorch_siege + double_siege + double_close + double_ranged + double_spies + optimize_agile_rows + restore_discard_to_hand) are implemented; nine leaders produce active executable `UseLeader` moves and six (King Bran + four cCp19 row-horn leaders + cCp20 Treacherous) are implemented passive without a `UseLeader` command. The active-command set grew from five (cCp14) to seven (cCp16) to eight (cCp21) to nine (cCp22) by adding Hope of the Aen Seidhe and Bringer of Death; the passive set grew from one (cCp15) to five (cCp19) to six (cCp20).

## Active Risks (cCp13 status)

- cCp13 resolved the three cCp12 follow-up risks. Light Longship now resolves as same-source Muster instead of no-opping. Roach now ships in every current/official preset containing Geralt or Cirilla, so `muster_roach` visibly resolves in normal starter play. Arachas Behemoth's one-way directionality is now actively asserted in catalog invariants and engine regressions rather than tracked as `it.todo`.
- After cCp17, only one `it.todo` remains in `tests/catalog/linkedAbilityInvariants.test.ts`, scoped to placeholder leader tutor/restore effects (Eredin, Emhyr, etc.). The generic `summon` discard-trigger todo was promoted to an active metadata-status invariant under the new `Summon discard-trigger lock (cCp17)` block; the engine regressions live in `tests/game/coreSummonDiscardTrigger.test.ts`. Promote the remaining placeholder when its underlying resolver lands.
- `skellige.hemdall` remains an ability-less stat-stick hero summoned only via Kambi's Avenger. cCp13 invariants assert this. If Hemdall ever grows an on-summon ability, the Avenger helper will need to invoke `resolveCardAbilities` on the replacement.
- All four `side_deck_only` sources (`neutral.bovine-defense-force`, `skellige.hemdall`, `skellige.vildkaarl`, `skellige.young-vildkaarl`) are linked from at least one current source; the existing invariants block accidental orphans.

## Active Risks (cCp12 status)

- cCp12 surfaced no `P0`/`P1` issues; the cCp10 reverse Gaunter miss is now locked down across catalog invariants (`tests/catalog/linkedAbilityInvariants.test.ts`), engine regressions (`tests/game/coreLinkedAbilities.test.ts`), and legacy regressions (`tests/game/legacyMuster.test.ts`).

## Update Requirements

- Every implementation phase must update this file.
- Every spec-only phase should update this file if it changes roadmap, status, or next step.
- CI enforces this on relevant pull requests through `npm run check:project-state`.
